Tricia Tan 8/08, Updated Ben Jones and Chioma Izzi-Engbeaya 11/13, Tricia Tan 08/14, Florian Wernig 11/15


CLONIDINE SUPPRESSION TEST

 

INDICATION

To exclude the diagnosis of phaeochromocytoma in patients with hypertension and borderline raised catecholamines or catecholamine metabolites.

 

CONTRAINDICATIONS

Frail patient with a history of hypotensive episodes/ severe coronary/ carotid disease.

 

SIDE EFFECTS

Hypotension and sedation

 

PREPARATION

•       Order the clonidine from pharmacy (readily obtainable).

•       Stop sympatholytic medications (eg. beta blockers) for at least 48 hours before the test.

•       Fast overnight.

•       Quiet environment.

•       Cancel test if baseline blood pressure is < 110/60 mmHg or in volume-depleted patients.

•       Sphyngomanometer or Critikon monitor.

•       i.v. Cannula, 18-20g.

•       Ice.

•       EDTA (purple) tubes.

•       Contact biochemistry laboratory before doing the test, enquire how they would like the samples taken and arrange for their delivery.

 

METHOD

1.         Insert i.v. cannula.

2.         Rest for 1/2 hour in supine position.

3.         Monitor BP and pulse at onset and every time blood taken.

4.         Take 2 baseline samples at 5 minute intervals.

5.         Give, at time zero, 300  micrograms (3 x 100mcg tablets) clonidine hydrochloride orally.

6.         Take blood for plasma metanephrines at 1, 2, 3 hours. Blood needs to be taken into EDTA, kept on ice, spun at 4°C, frozen until assay.

 

 

 

 

 

 

INTERPRETATION

•       Clonidine (alpha2-adrenoceptor agonist) acts via the alpha pre-ganglionic receptors to reduce catecholamine secretion in patients without phaechromocytoma or paraganglioma, but not in patients with autonomous tumoural secretion of catecholamines by a phaeochromocytoma or paraganglioma.

•       An abnormal result indicative of a phaechromocytoma or paraganglioma consists of an elevation of plasma normetanephrine at 3 hours after clonidine administration and a less than 40% decrease in plasma normetanephrine levels compared with baseline

 

SENSITIVITY AND SPECIFICITY (Eisenhofer, et al):

*Elevated norepinephrine after clonidine AND lack of suppression (>50%):

Sensitivity 67%, Specificity 98%

Elevated normetanephrine after clonidine AND lack of suppression (>40%):

Sensitivity 96%, Specificity 100%

Elevated normetanephrine after clonidine OR lack of suppression (>40%):

Sensitivity 96%, Specificity 67%

 

*No longer recommended for diagnosis

 

REFERENCES

Bravo et al., (1981) NEJM 305:623

Grossman et al., (1991) Hypertension 17:733

Eisenhofer et al. (2003) JCEM 88 : 2856

 

VERSION HISTORY

Stephen Gilbey 1/90; revised Tricia Tan 5/08. Florian Wernig 11/15

 

PENTOLINIUM SUPPRESSION TEST

This test is nearly obsolete as it is difficult to obtain pentolinium.

 

INDICATION

1.      To exclude the diagnosis of phaeochromocytoma in patients with hypertension and borderline changes in plasma catecholamines or 24 hour urinary catecholamines.

 

CONTRAINDICATIONS

No absolute contraindications but beware frail patient and patients with severe coronary or carotid vascular disease.

 

SIDE EFFECTS

May cause severe transient hypotension.

 

PREPARATION

•       Order the Pentolinium from pharmacy allowing a week for pharmacy to order in (difficult to obtain). It comes as 10 mg in 2 ml.

•       Stop hypotensive treatment (incuding labetalol) for at least 24 hours before the test, especially centrally acting drugs such as methyldopa.

•       Fast overnight. (Large meals can cause variation in catecholamines).

•       Quiet environment.

•       Sphygmomanometer or Critikon BP monitor.

•       Cannula, 18-20g.

•       Ice.

•       Lithium heparin tubes (green top Vacutainer).

•       Contact biochemistry laboratory before doing the test, enquire how they would like the samples taken and arrange for their delivery.

 

METHOD

1.      Patient should empty bladder before lying down as they might not be allowed to stand for a while.

2.      Insert cannula and flush.

3.      Rest for 1/2 hour.

4.      Monitor BP and pulse at onset and every time blood taken.

6.      Take 2 baseline samples at 5 minute intervals for catecholamines. Blood needs to be taken into Lithium heparin, kept on ice, spun at 4°C, frozen until assay.

7.      At time 0, give 2.5 mg Pentolinium i.v.

8.      Take blood at one hour.

 

 

INTERPRETATION

•       Pentolinium is a sympathetic ganglion blocker. Normal subjects may show an initially elevated plasma adrenaline and noradrenaline but these will fall to within the normal plasma range with Pentolinium.

•       In contrast the autonomous secretion of a phaeochromocytoma will not suppress.

 

SENSITIVITY AND SPECIFICITY

This test has a low false positive and false negative rate as determined in series of known phaeochromocytomas and normals but published information is very scanty. The most likely theoretical problem is a fall in plasma catecholamine levels in a phaeochromocytoma patient whose tumour is only secreting episodically.

 

REFERENCES

Brown et al., (1981) Lancet 1:174

 

VERSION HISTORY

Stephen Gilbey 1/90; revised Bernard Khoo, Karim Meeran, Rachel Batterham and Jeannie Todd 7/00.

 

 

 

123I- META-IODOBENZYLGUANIDINE (MIBG) SCAN

 

INDICATION

The MIBG scan is a useful, qualitative, method of locating the site of a phaeochromocytoma.

It should not be undertaken without biochemical evidence for a tumour being present (urine or plasma catecholamines/metanephrines), and should be backed up by ultrasound, CT scanning, and, where indicated, venous sampling. It is particularly useful for extra-adrenal and metastatic or residual phaeochromocytoma.

 

CONTRAINDICATIONS

No absolute contraindications except pregnancy, allergy to iodine.

Caution in any patient with any drug allergies.

Many drugs may interfere with the study – nuclear scanning have a list. These include tricyclic antidepressants, SSRIs, calcium channel antagonists, catecholamine receptor agonists and antagonists, phenothiazines, butyrophenones (e.g. haloperidol etc.), guanethidine and reserpine.

 

PREPARATION

•       Liase with nuclear medicine at least 1 week in advance of planned scan.

•       Avoid i.v. phenoxybenzamine (may lead to false negative result), although oral phenoxybenzamine is OK.

•       Thyroid uptake should be blocked by potassium iodide 60 mg bd for 48 hours beforehand and for 5 days afterwards.

 

METHOD

1.      [123]I-MIBG is injected intravenously (this molecule is similar to noradrenaline, transported in similar fashion and stored in catecholamine vesicles).

2.      The patient is scanned twice at 24 and 48 hours.

 

INTERPRETATION

•       Spots of increased uptake on scanning are the tumours.

•       Most common sites: adrenals, organ of Zuckerkandl (usually pelvis).

•       Tumours can be anywhere from base of skull to bifurcation of aorta. Sometimes found near bladder and rarely in the prostate.

•       May be multiple.

•       If the MIBG is negative but there is compelling biochemical evidence of a catecholamine secreting tumour, consider [18]F-FDG or [68]Ga-DOTATATE PET scanning.

 

SENSITIVITY AND SPECIFICITY

In patients with a proven phaeochromocytoma but uncertain site the scan has 90-96% sensitivity and 98-99% specificity. More recent data with a large series of patients shows that the sensitivity is only 75%. Tumour detection was lower for extra-adrenal paragangliomas (58%) versus adrenal (85%) phaeochromocytomas.

 

REFERENCES

Sisson et al., (1981) New Engl J Med 305:12

Shapiro et al., (1985) Cardiology 72 suppl. 1:137

Win et al., (2007) Nuclear Medicine Communications 28:359

Bhatia et al., (2008) Clin Endo 69:181

 

VERSION HISTORY

Stephen Gilbey 11/89, revised Tricia Tan 05/08

 

 

GENETIC SCREENING FOR PHAEOCHROMOCYTOMAS

 

INDICATION

At least 30% of patients with phaeochromocytoma/paraganglioma (PH/PGL) disease have a germline mutation in SDHB (10%), SDHD (9%), VHL (7%), RET (6%), and NF1 (3%). Frequencies of less than 2% of germline mutations are found for SDHC, SDHA, MAX, and TMEM127. SDHB mutations lead to metastatic disease in 40% or more of affected patients.

The following clinical details are useful:

 

•        Family history: especially with VHL, RET, NF1, SDHD. There is genomic imprinting of SDHD: the disease phenotype is only apparent in patients who have inherited the mutant allele from their father, as a rule (although there is one exception in the literature). Family history may not be apparent as most of the genes except NF1 (and possibly RET) are not fully penetrant. All disorders have the potential to be de novo, so the absence of family history does not reduce risks.

•        Age at presentation: Hereditary catecholamine-producing tumours typically present at a younger age than sporadic neoplasms. Sporadic phaeochromocytomas are often diagnosed on the basis of symptoms or following incidental discovery on imaging, whereas hereditary phaeochromocytomas are frequently diagnosed earlier in the course of the disease because of biochemical screening/surveillance or genetic testing. This of course is only the case if the individual is known to have a predisposition.

•        Bilateral and multifocal disease: bilateral PH disease is associated with c-Ret or VHL disease. Multifocal PGL disease is associated with SDHB and SDHD disease. Head and neck tumours are associated with SDHD disease.

•        Malignancy: defined as metastases to non-chromaffin tissue (e.g. bone, lungs, liver, lymph nodes). This is particularly associated with SDH-B disease.

•        Clinical features of neurofibromatosis: NF1 disease (autosomal dominant) is a clinical diagnosis based on the presence of multiple café-au-lait spots, neurofibromas, Lisch nodules (iris hamartomas), central nervous system gliomas, and axillary freckling. DNA analysis is not routinely required.

•        Clinical features of VHL disease: CNS and retinal haemangioblastomas, renal cell carcinomas, pancreatic neuroendocrine tumours, endolymphatic sac tumours of the middle ear, serous cystadenomas of the pancrease, and papillary cystadenomas of the epididymis and broad ligament.

•        Clinical features of MEN 2: Medullary thyroid carcinoma, parathyroid hyperplasia (2A), mucosal neuromas (2B), marfanoid phenotype (2B).

 


REFERRAL TO THE JOINT ENDOCRINE/GENETICS CLINIC

Can be done directly for any patients (though patients who live far from Hammersmith should be given a choice to be seen more locally):

•        Patients presenting with phaeochromocytoma ≤ 50 years old.

•        Patients with extra-adrenal PGL, metastatic disease or multiple tumours at any age.

•        Patients with tumour at any age, with a personal or family history of similar, or of related tumours (eg MTC, haemangioblastoma, renal cell cancer)

•        Relatives of an index patient with a known mutation, for cascade screening.

Genetic counselling and sampling will be handled by the clinic. It is not possible to offer primary genetic testing in the relative of an individual with PH/PGL if that person is not themselves affected.

METHODS

If you want to arrange your own genetic testing rather than going through the Genetic clinic, Birmingham offer a panel of genes SDHB, SDHC, SDHD, SDHAF2, RET, TMEM127, MAX, VHL and FH for £750, with results in around 4 months of receipt of sample. Exeter and some other labs offer a similar panel for similar cost and turn-around-time. Single gene mutation analysis is not typically available any longer (except in the case of a known familial mutation). Samples should be sent to West Midlands Regional Genetics Laboratory, Birmingham Women’s Hospital, Metchley Park Road, Edgbaston, Birmingham, B15 2TG. Tel: 0121 627 2710 Fax: 0121 627 2711.

Ensure that you have discussed with the patient the potential of making a diagnosis, which puts them and their children at risk of multiple tumours other than phaeochromocytoma, to guarantee fully informed consent.

 

SDHA is extremely difficult to analyse due to multiple pseudogenes, and is only offered on consultation with the lab, with very good evidence.

 

REFERENCES

Gimenez-Roqueplo et al., (2006) Clin Endocrinol (Oxf) 65:699

Pacak K et al., (2007) Nature Clin Pract Endocrinol Metab 3:92

Lenders et al., (2014) JCEM 99:1915

 

VERSION HISTORY

Revised Tricia Tan 5/08, Florian Wernig & Natalie Canham 11/15

 

 

 

 

 

 


PERIOPERATIVE MANAGEMENT OF PHAEOCHROMOCYTOMAS

ALPHA BLOCKADE

Phenoxybenzamine is a non-competitive, non-selective inhibitor with a relatively long duration of action. Inhibition of alpha-adrenergic receptors by phenoxybenzamine is not overcome by extra catecholamine release intra-operatively. Therefore, there is reduced risk of intra-operative hypertensive crisis. However, this irreversible binding can lead to increased risk of post-operative hypotension.

Doxazosin is a competitive, selective α-1 receptor blocker with a relatively short duration of action. Due to its competitive nature, it can be overcome by extra catecholamine release intra-operatively with a potentially increased risk of hypertensive crisis. However, the shorter half-life makes it less likely to cause post-operative hypotension.

 

There is no conclusive evidence to favour the use of selective α-1 adrenergic receptor blockers (e.g. doxazosin/prazosin) over non-selective α receptor blockers (e.g. phenoxybenzamine). A retrospective study of Mayo versus Cleveland experience showed that use of phenoxybenzamine is associated with less intra-operative hypertension and more post-operative hypotension. However, there was no difference in post-operative surgical outcomes or length of hospital stay. Other studies have found no differences in intra-operative or post-operative blood pressures.

 

BETA BLOCKADE

There is no evidence to support the preference of selective β1 adrenergic receptor blockers over non-selective β receptor blockers.

Beta-blockers must NOT be started before alpha blockade because of the risk of hypertensive crisis. This is due to α-adrenergic receptor mediated vasoconstriction without the opposition of the β2-adrenergic receptor mediated vasodilation.

 

PRE-OPERATIVE MANAGEMENT

Patients require pre-operative treatment for a minimum of 14 days to normalise blood pressure (BP) for elective surgery. Where surgery for whatever reason is urgent, one needs to balance the risk of shorter pre-op treatments with the benefits of earlier surgery. Phentolamine (see below) can be used for very rapid control of blood pressure, but be aware of the risks of sudden acute falls in blood pressure. Modern non-ionic IV contrast does not cause a significant increase in catecholamine levels, so blockade is usually not necessary for CT scanning.

 

•        All patients should have an ECG and an echocardiogram (to evaluate for the presence of cardiomyopathy).

 

•        Start Phenoxybenzamine 10 mg once nocte. Increase every 3 days by 10-20 mg.

 

Aim for:

Target BP: Sitting BP < 130/80 and Standing Systolic BP >90 mmHg.

Target HR: 60-80bpm.

Aim for a final dose of phenoxybenzamine of 1.5mg/kg per day prior to surgery.

Warn patients about postural dizziness, nasal stuffiness, fatigue and retrograde ejaculation in men.

 

•        On the second day of alpha-adrenergic blockade, patients need to start a high sodium intake (>5 g daily) and adequate oral hydration (3 L per day) to reverse catecholamine-induced blood volume contraction and prevent hypotension. Caution in patients with heart or renal failure. Occasionally patients may require intravenous 0.9% saline if they continue to have postural hypotension.

 

•        Start Propranolol 20 mg tds, once heart rate >80 bpm. This can be titrated up to 40 mg tds. An alternative is to use Atenolol (25-50 mg).

Warn patients about side effects including cold peripheries, fatigue and dizziness

 

•        Calcium channel blockers (e.g. Amlodipine 5-10 mg od) may be added if BP is not controlled.

 

•        Intravenous 0.9% saline (2-3 L over 24 hr period) should be commenced on admission (2 days pre-operatively, hence if surgery is on Monday, this should start on Saturday). Caution in patients with heart or renal failure.

•        Continue intravenous fluids post-operatively, as hypotension may result due to residual effects of pre-operative alpha blockade for 24 to 48 hours.

 

•        Monitor BP, HR and capillary blood glucose (to detect rebound hypoglycaemia) for up to 48 hours post-operatively.

NB. Patients may remain hypertensive for a few days post-operatively.

 

•        Lifelong biochemical follow-up is necessary. Measure 24-hour urine metanephrines at 6 weeks, 6 months and then annually thereafter.

 

HYPERTENSIVE CRISIS

In the event of a crisis, use intravenous phentolamine (0.5–1 mg) boluses, which can be repeated until BP controlled. Note that phentolamine is supplied as 10mg per ml, so very tiny volumes are required to bring down blood pressure. This works rapidly. If patients are not well filled, this can precipitate severe hypotension and a watershed cerebral infarction. Rehydration with crystalloids MUST therefore be started at the same time.

 

REFERENCES

Kinney et al., (2002) J Cardiothorac Vasc Anesth 16:359

Kocak et al., (2002) Int Surg 87:191

Weingarten et al., (2010) Urology 76:508.e6-11

Fishbein et al., (2013) J Clin Hypertens 15:428

Lenders et al., (2014) J Clin Endocrinol Metab 99:1915

Amir Sam and Sarah Ali 06/15

 

9번역 완료

갑상선 (THYROID)

방사성요오드 치료 프로토콜 (PROTOCOL FOR RADIO-IODINE TREATMENT)

우리 기관에서 시행한 최근 연구에서, 갑상선기능항진증 환자에게 보조적으로 lithium을 투여하면 방사성요오드 치료 후 완치 가능성이 60% 높았다(Martin et al., (2012) Clin Endo 77:621). 아래 프로토콜은 lithium을 포함하지만, lithium 사용이 필수는 아니며 lithium을 빼고 프로토콜을 따를 수도 있다.

시점 내용
−7일 갑상선기능검사. Charing Cross Hospital 핵의학과에서 설명 및 동의서 서명. 환자의 GP에게 안내 서신 발송. 항갑상선제 중단.
−3일 Lithium carbonate(Priadel) 800 mg 취침 전 시작.
0일 갑상선기능검사. Lithium 농도. 방사성요오드 치료.
+1주 갑상선기능검사. Lithium 농도. Lithium 치료 중단.
+3주 갑상선기능검사. Lithium 농도.
+6주 갑상선기능검사.
+9주 갑상선기능검사.
+12주 갑상선기능검사.
+14주 외래 방문.

환자에게 무엇을 주어야 하는가? (WHAT DO YOU NEED TO GIVE THE PATIENT?)

  1. 방사성요오드 치료 안내문: http://www.imperialendo.com/for-patients/patent-information
  2. Charing Cross의 Meeran D5 Endo Radioiodine F/UP 목록으로 예약을 잡아준다. 나머지(아래)는 참고용이며 그 클리닉에서 처리한다.

환자는 다음을 받게 된다.

  1. 핵의학과 I-131 치료용 X-Ray 의뢰서.
  2. TFT(TSH, fT4, fT3) 의뢰서 7장(위와 같이 −1, 0, +1, +3, +6, +9, +12주).
  3. Lithium 농도 의뢰서 2장(위와 같이 0주와 +1주).
  4. Lithium 800 mg(Priadel로 명시) 취침 전 10일분 처방.
  5. Thyroxine 100 μg 처방 — 전화 연락을 받기 전까지는 시작하지 않는다.
  6. 치료 14주 후 외래 예약.
  7. 기록지와 X-Ray 의뢰서에 환자 전화번호를 반드시 적는다. 결과/치료에 관해 문의해야 할 경우 대표번호와 bleep 4998을 알려준다.

방사성요오드 치료 후 전화 클리닉 프로토콜 (PROTOCOL FOR THE POST-RADIOIODINE TREATMENT TELEPHONE CLINIC)

목표 (AIM)

방사성요오드 치료 후 우리의 목표는 환자를 정상 갑상선 상태로 만드는 것이며, 갑상선기능저하증도 받아들일 수 있다. 전화 클리닉의 목적은 thyroxine 보충을 언제 시작할지 신속히 결정하여, 불필요한 외래 방문을 피하고, 갑상선 안병증 악화 가능성 등 바람직하지 않은 영향이 많은 갑상선기능저하증을 치료하지 않은 채 방치하지 않는 것이다. http://radioiodine.info 참조.

전화 추적에 적합한 환자 (WHO IS SUITABLE FOR TELEPHONE FOLLOW-UP?)

이 서비스는 다음 조건을 갖춘 환자만 이용할 수 있다.

  • 업무 시간(오전 9시–오후 5시)에 전화 연락이 가능하다.
  • 어느 정도 영어를 할 수 있고 약물 지시를 따를 수 있다.
  • 정기적인 혈액검사, 가능하면 병원 채혈실에서의 검사에 동의한다.

방사성요오드 치료는 어떻게 준비하는가? (HOW DO I ARRANGE FOR RADIO-IODINE TREATMENT?)

환자가 방사성요오드에 적합할 수 있다고 판단되면, 외래 서신 사본을 cc로 보내거나 이메일(zarni.win@imperial.nhs.uk)을 보내 Dr Zarni Win(Charing Cross Hospital 영상의학과 전문의)에게 의뢰한다. 이것이 환자가 방사성요오드를 받는다는 뜻은 아니며, 이 치료 선택지를 논의하기 위한 전문 상담을 받아야 한다는 뜻이다. 특히 방사선 방호 문제는 일반화할 수 없으므로 환자별로 개별 논의해야 한다.

방사성요오드를 쓰기로 결정하면 즉시 항갑상선제를 중단해야 한다. 이상적으로는 최소 1주간 중단한다. 환자가 대략 언제 방사성요오드를 원하는지 알면 추가 계획을 세울 수 있다. 환자가 치료를 2개월 넘게 미룰 것이 분명하면, 방사성요오드 날짜에 가까워질 때까지 항갑상선제를 지속한다. 지침으로서 항갑상선제는 방사성요오드 최소 1주 전, 최대 2개월 전에 중단한다. 증상 조절에는 beta-blocker를 사용할 수 있다.

활동성 갑상선 안병증이 있으면 방사성요오드 치료 시 steroid cover에 관한 정보는 갑상선 안병증 항목도 참조한다.

가능한 한 빨리 Charing Cross Hospital의 Meeran D5 Endo Radioiodine F/UP 목록으로 예약을 잡아준다. Hammersmith Hospital에 있다면 Meeran D5 Endo Radioiodine F/UP 예약을 잡아주고, 표지에도 적어 실제로 Charing Cross 시스템에 예약되도록 해야 한다.

의뢰서를 보낸다(또는 http://meeran.info의 웹 양식 작성).

  • Radioiodine clinic (c/o Prof. Meeran)
  • c/o Dept of Endocrinology
  • 9th Floor, East Wing, Charing Cross Hospital (Fax: 020 33 111862)

서신에 연락 가능한 전화번호를 가능한 한 많이 적는다.

누가 전화로 추적하는가? (WHO WILL FOLLOW PATIENTS UP BY TELEPHONE?)

지정된 의사가 전산 결과를 매주 갱신·검토하며, 치료 후 생화학적으로 갑상선기능저하 또는 항진 상태가 되면 환자에게 전화한다.

방사성요오드 후 의미 있는 증상이 생기면? (WHAT HAPPENS IF A PATIENT DEVELOPS SIGNIFICANT SYMPTOMS AFTER RADIOIODINE?)

어느 단계에서든 환자에게 증상이 생기거나 Graves 안병증이 생기면, 환자나 GP가 내분비내과에 연락할 수 있으며 다음 외래에서 긴급히 진료하도록 조치한다.

Thyroxine은 언제 시작하는가? (WHEN WILL THYROXINE BE STARTED?)

방사성요오드 투여 당일과 1주 후에 lithium 농도 확인을 위해 채혈한다. 갑상선기능검사도 모니터링하지만 조치는 취하지 않는다(lithium에 의해 fT4가 낮아질 수 있으므로).

fT4가 14.5 pmol/L 이하로 떨어지면 전화로 thyroxine을 시작한다. 3, 6, 9, 12주에 채혈하고 약 14주에 외래를 방문한다. 여전히 갑상선중독 상태이면 15, 18, 21주에 추가 전화 진료를 잡을 수 있다.

재평가(12주 후) 시 TSH가 여전히 측정되지 않으면 thyroxine 과다이거나 방사성요오드 실패이다. fT3 >5 pmol/L이면 자율 기능을 시사하므로 thyroxine을 중단한다. fT3 <5.0 pmol/L이면 3주 간격 전화 진료로 thyroxine 용량을 서서히 줄일 수 있다.

전화 클리닉 담당자를 위한 지침 (INSTRUCTIONS FOR THOSE RUNNING THE TELEPHONE CLINIC)

환자는 thyroxine을 시작할지 여부를 알려주는 전화를 기다리고 있다.

먼저 전산에서 fT3/fT4/TSH 결과를 확인한다.

환자에게 체중을 kg으로 묻는다(또는 stone/lbs로 듣고 환산).

환자의 상태를 확인한다. 특히 오심에 대해 묻는다. Lithium을 제대로 복용했는지 확인한다. 몇 알을 먹었는가? 어느 것을 빠뜨렸는가?

환자가 갑상선중독 증상(떨림, 빈맥 등)을 느끼고 fT4 >40 pmol/L이면, 여전히 갑상선기능저하증으로 빠르게 진행할 수 있으므로 다음(3주 후) 평가까지 propranolol(예: 20–40 mg 1일 3회)을 권한다.

fT4 <14.6 pmol/L이면 thyroxine 100 mcg 1일 1회를 시작한다. 환자는 집에 약을 가지고 있으므로 전화한 당일부터 시작할 수 있어야 한다.

12주 후 TSH가 억제되어 있으면 fT3를 확인한다. fT3 >5 pmol/L이면 thyroxine을 중단한다. fT3 <5 pmol/L이면 thyroxine을 서서히 줄인다.

갑상선 클리닉에서 전화할 때마다 이 페이지를 작성하여 갑상선 파일에 보관한다.

  • 이름:
  • 병원 등록번호:
  • 전화 날짜:
  • 채혈 날짜:
  • 방사성요오드(RAI) 날짜:
  • RAI 후 경과 주수:
  • 결과: fT4 / fT3 / TSH
  • Lithium 농도 검사 여부(예/아니오). 결과는 확인하지 않는다.
  • 전화 내용:
  • 환자 상태에 관한 의견:
  • 환자 체중(집에서 측정).
  • Lithium을 몇 알 복용했고 몇 알을 잊었는가? 어느 것을 잊었는가? (정확한 날짜 확인)
  • 흡연하는가? 하루 몇 개비?
  • 현재 복용 중인 약:
  • fT4 <14.6 pmol/L이면 thyroxine 100 mcg 1일 1회를 시작한다. 안내 내용: thyroxine 시작 또는 추가 채혈까지 대기:
  • fT4 >30 pmol/L이고 환자가 불편감(떨림, 빈맥 등)을 느끼면 propranolol을 고려한다.
  • 12주 후 TSH가 억제되어 있으면 fT3를 확인한다. fT3 >5 pmol/L이면 thyroxine을 중단한다. fT3 <5 pmol/L이면 thyroxine을 서서히 줄인다.

갑상선 안병증과 방사성요오드 (THYROID EYE DISEASE AND RADIOIODINE)

갑상선 안병증 악화는 주로 흡연자에서 일어나며, 흡연자의 안병증 악화 위험은 약 25%이다. 대규모 RCT에서 흡연자는 갑상선기능항진증 치료 방법과 무관하게 갑상선 안병증의 진행 또는 새로운 발생 위험이 높았으나, 방사성요오드로 치료한 흡연자의 위험이 가장 높았다. 후향적 짝지은 코호트 연구에서, 방사성요오드 투여 1일 후 시작하여 6주간 지속한 저용량 prednisolone(0.2 mg/kg 체중)이 부작용이 매우 적으면서 효과적이었다. 대다수에서는 steroid를 피하고, 안 징후가 악화되는 소수만 그 시점에 steroid로 치료하는 것이 바람직하다.

따라서 비흡연자에서 예방적 steroid 사용의 적응증은 없다. 의문이 있으면 모든 환자를 Vickie Lee(Charing Cross Hospital 안과 전문의)에게 의뢰한다.

참고문헌 (REFERENCES)

Bartalena et al., (1998) Ann Int Med 129:632

Traisk F, et al., (2009) J Clin Endocrinol Metab 94:3700

Lai et al., (2010) J Clin Endocrinol Metab 95:1333

개정 이력 (VERSION HISTORY)

Elaine Murphy 07/01, Karim Meeran 01/03, Florian Wernig and Chioma Izzi-Engbeaya 11/13, Saira Hameed 04/15

갑상선절제술을 위한 갑상선중독증 환자의 준비 (PREPARATION OF THYROTOXIC PATIENTS FOR THYROIDECTOMY)

환자는 이상적으로 수술 전에 정상 갑상선 상태가 되어야 한다. 어떤 경우든 수술 지연의 위험과 수술 전 정상 갑상선 상태의 이득을 항상 저울질해야 한다. 응급 상황에서는 다음 약물 조합으로 약 10일 만에 이를 달성하도록 시도할 수 있다.

  1. Beta 차단. 전통적으로 propranolol 80 mg 1일 3회를 선호한다. Atenolol 100 mg 또는 nadolol 160 mg은 1일 1회만 투여하면 되므로 순응도를 높일 수 있다.

    [역자 주] 원문은 “nadolol 16 0mg”으로 표기되어 있으며 160 mg의 오타로 보인다.

  2. Propylthiouracil(PTU) 250 mg 1일 4회. 평소보다 높은 용량이지만, 응급 상황에서는 간에서 이 약물의 대사가 증가하므로 더 높은 용량을 더 자주 투여해야 한다. PTU는 투여한 요오드의 유기화를 막으므로 요오드화물 치료보다 1시간 먼저 투여해야 한다.

  3. 과량의 요오드화물 또는 요오드(KI 65 mg 1일 3회 또는 Lugol’s iodine 0.3 mL 1일 3회). Potassium iodide 65 mg 1일 3회가 단연 투여하기 쉬우며 Lugol처럼 맛이 고약하지 않다(NEJM (1980) 302;883-885).

갑상선 전절제술 또는 완결 갑상선절제술 후 thyroxine 시작 (STARTING THYROXINE POST-TOTAL OR COMPLETION THYROIDECTOMY)

갑상선 전절제술 또는 완결 갑상선절제술 후에는 체중에 근거한 추정으로 thyroxine을 시작한다. 일반적인 용량은 1.5–2 mcg/kg이다. 고령자에서는 심방세동 위험을 최소화하기 위해 1.5 mcg/kg 쪽으로 정한다.

갑상선 안병증 관리 프로토콜 (PROTOCOL FOR THE MANAGEMENT OF THYROID EYE DISEASE (TED))

다음 중 하나라도 있으면 안과에 긴급 의뢰한다 (REFER URGENTLY TO OPHTHALMOLOGY IF ANY OF THE FOLLOWING ARE PRESENT)

증상:

  • 설명되지 않는 시력 저하
  • 한쪽 또는 양쪽 눈에서 색각의 강도/질 변화 자각
  • 눈이 갑자기 ‘튀어나온’ 병력(안구 아탈구)

징후:

  • 뚜렷한 각막 혼탁
  • 눈꺼풀을 감아도 각막이 보임
  • 시신경유두 부종

다음 중 하나라도 있으면 안과에 비긴급 의뢰한다 (REFER NON-URGENTLY TO OPHTHALMOLOGY IF ANY OF THE FOLLOWING ARE PRESENT)

증상:

  • 빛에 비정상적으로 예민한 눈(눈부심): 지난 12개월간 불편하거나 악화됨
  • 눈의 이물감이 심하고 국소 윤활제 1주 사용 후에도 호전되지 않음
  • 눈 안 또는 눈 뒤의 통증: 지난 12개월간 불편하거나 악화됨
  • 지난 1–2개월간 눈 및/또는 눈꺼풀 모양의 진행성 변화
  • 복시

징후:

  • 불편한 눈꺼풀 후퇴
  • 눈꺼풀 또는 결막의 비정상적 부종이나 발적
  • 안구 운동 제한 또는 현성 사시
  • 복시를 피하기 위해 고개를 기울임

임상 활성도 점수 (CLINICAL ACTIVITY SCORE, CAS)

  • 안정 시 통증 — 1
  • 안구 운동 시 통증 — 1
  • 결막 발적 — 1
  • 눈꺼풀 발적 — 1
  • 눈물언덕(caruncle) 부종 — 1
  • 결막 부종(chemosis) — 1
  • 눈꺼풀 부종 — 1

CAS ≥3이면 1차 치료가 필요할 가능성이 높은 활동성 질환을 나타낸다.

중증도 등급 (SEVERITY GRADING)

  • 1등급 — 안구 자극
  • 2등급 — 연조직 부종 또는 안구돌출
  • 3등급 — 안구 운동 제한
  • 4등급 — 시신경병증 또는 노출 각막병증

관리 프로토콜 (MANAGEMENT PROTOCOL) (EUGOGO)

[역자 주] 원문에는 이 제목 아래에 EUGOGO 관리 알고리즘 그림이 있었던 것으로 보이나, 제공된 워드 파일에는 그림이 포함되어 있지 않다.

갑상선 안병증의 일반적 관리 (GENERAL MANAGEMENT OF TED)

  • 금연 프로그램.
  • 정상 갑상선 상태 회복.
  • 갑상선 상태를 면밀히 모니터링.
  • 갑상선 안병증이 활동성이면 방사성요오드 치료에 steroid cover를 병행해야 하며, steroid는 방사성요오드 투여 당일 시작한다.
    • Prednisolone 0.2 mg/kg 용량을 권한다.
    • 같은 용량을 6주간 지속한 뒤 한 번에 중단한다(즉 감량하지 않음).
  • 비활동성 안정 갑상선 안병증은 steroid cover가 필요 없다.
  • 방사성요오드 후 갑상선기능저하증을 피한다.
  • Selenium 보충.

경증 갑상선 안병증 (MILD TED, GRADE I/II SEVERITY)

  • 증상
    • 경미한 눈꺼풀 후퇴 및/또는 안구돌출(<3 mm) 징후를 동반한 안구 자극.
  • 임상 활성도 점수 3/7 미만의 경미한 활성도로, 경미한 연조직 부종을 포함한다(간헐적 복시, 단속운동 불일치).
  • 활동성이라도 대개 전신 면역억제가 필요하지 않으며, 안구 표면 개선으로 관리할 수 있는 경우가 많다.
  • 윤활제로는 Clinitas, Hyloforte, Xailin HA 모두 적절하다.
  • 마땅한 것이 없으면 단순 carmellose(예: Celluvisc)로 충분하다. 야간에는 Xailin Night나 Lacrimal Lube 같은 paraffin 기반 연고가 좋다.

중등도–중증 갑상선 안병증 (MODERATE TO SEVERE TED, GRADE III SEVERITY)

  • 임상 활성도 >3/7:
  • 안구돌출 >3 mm, 연조직 부종(눈꺼풀 부종이나 홍반, 결막 부종이나 충혈, 눈물언덕 부종), 통증(안정 시 또는 안구 운동 시), 복시(지속적, 비지속적).
  • 활동성이고 일상생활에 의미 있는 영향이 있으면 전신 면역억제(예: 정맥 glucocorticoid — 아래 참조)가 필요하다.
  • 비활동성 질환은 수술적 재건(눈꺼풀 내림술, 사시 수술 또는 안와 감압술)이 필요할 수 있다.

시력을 위협하는 갑상선 안병증 (SIGHT THREATENING TED, GRADE IV)

시신경병증 또는 노출 각막병증에 의한 각막 손상은 즉각적 중재 — 내과적 또는 수술적 감압 — 가 필요하다.

내과적 관리 (MEDICAL MANAGEMENT)

아래 내용은 정보/참고용일 뿐이다.

모든 전신 면역억제는 갑상선 안병증을 전문으로 하는 안과 의료진이 결정하며, 필요하면 류마티스내과의 지원을 받는다.

Methylprednisolone 1 g 정맥 펄스를 3일 연속 매일 투여(대개 PIU에서)한 후, 250 mg 정맥 주 1회 또는 경구 prednisolone 0.7–1 mg/kg을 지속한다.

± 2차 면역억제.

중등도–중증 갑상선 안병증의 경우 (FOR MODERATE TO SEVERE TED)

Methylprednisolone 500 mg 정맥 펄스를 주 1회 6주간 투여한 뒤, 250 mg 주 1회를 6주 더 투여한다. 소아에는 적합하지 않다.

Steroid에 대한 조기 반응이 치료 결과를 예측한다. 결과는 질환 기간과 반비례한다.

중증도에 따라 안과에서 류마티스내과의 지원을 받아 2차 면역억제를 고려할 수 있다.

방사선치료 (RADIOTHERAPY)

활동성 갑상선 안병증 치료에서 방사선치료와 steroid 병용이 steroid 단독보다 효과적이지만, 2차 면역억제와 steroid 병용이 방사선치료와 steroid 병용보다 효과적인지는 알려져 있지 않다. CIRTED 연구 결과가 어느 한 치료 요법을 지지하는 추가 근거를 제공할 것이다.

수술적 관리 (SURGICAL MANAGEMENT)

안와 감압술.

참고문헌 (REFERENCES)

EUGOGO (2008) Eur J Endocrinol 158:273

Bartalena et al., (1997) Clin Endocrinol (Oxf) 47:9

Mourits et al., (2011) New Engl J Med 364:1920

개정 이력 (VERSION HISTORY)

Mano Sira 04/15

갑상선 수질암에 대한 pentagastrin 검사 (PENTAGASTRIN TEST FOR MEDULLARY THYROID CARCINOMA)

Calcitonin (CT)

Calcitonin은 갑상선의 C세포에서 분비될 수 있다. 높은 수치는 갑상선 수질암을 시사할 수 있다. 매우 초기 질환(예: 가족성 증후군 선별 시)에서는 수치가 상승하지 않을 수 있으나 calcium이나 pentagastrin으로 자극될 수 있다. 경계선 기저 수치(이제는 공복 채혈이 필요 없음)는 자극검사로 추가 평가할 것을 권한다.

적응증 (INDICATIONS)

  1. 갑상선 수질암 의심.
  2. 갑상선 수질암 가족의 선별검사.
  3. MEN 2형 의심 환자. MEN2 등록부에 알리는 것을 잊지 말 것.
  4. 기저 CT 수치(남성 <11.8, 여성 <4.8 ng/L)인 환자.

[역자 주] 원문 4번 항목은 “Patients with basal CT level (< 11.8 (male) and < 4.8(female) ngLl) /L/L”로 표기가 깨져 있다. 기저 CT가 이 참고 상한 근처(경계선)인 환자를 가리키는 것으로 보인다.

금기 (CONTRAINDICATIONS)

알레르기/아나필락시스.

부작용 (SIDE EFFECTS)

짧게 지속되는 심한 오심, 상복부 불편감.

준비 (PREPARATION)

  1. 전해질과 혈청 calcium을 확인한다.
  2. 정맥 cannula, 18–20 G.
  3. Pentagastrin 0.5 mcg/kg 체중. Pentagastrin은 500 mcg/2 mL 제형이다. 따라서 체중 100 kg 환자에게는 50 mcg, 즉 0.2 mL만 필요하다.
  4. 빨간색 또는 노란색 마개 튜브 6개.
  5. 원심분리기.
  6. 얼음 또는 검체를 즉시 검사실로 보낼 수 있는 시설.

방법 (METHOD)

  1. 음식은 calcitonin을 높일 수 있으므로 환자는 금식해야 한다.
  2. Cannula를 삽입하고 flush한다.
  3. Calcitonin 기저 검체를 채취한다.
  4. Pentagastrin은 일부 환자에게 몇 초간 매우 불편한 느낌을 주므로, 투여 전 환자가 누워 있는지 확인한다.
  5. Pentagastrin 0.5 mcg/kg 체중을 정맥 bolus로 투여하고 cannula를 flush한다.
  6. 1, 2, 3, 5, 10분에 calcitonin 검체를 채취한다.
  7. 얼음에 담아 즉시 검사실로 보낸다.

해석 (INTERPRETATION)

  • 자극 후 CT 30–100 ng/L: 추적 선별 권고.
  • 자극 후 CT 100–200 ng/L: C세포 과형성 또는 초기 MTC 가능성.
  • 자극 후 CT >200 ng/L: MTC 가능성 매우 높음.

민감도와 특이도 (SENSITIVITY AND SPECIFICITY)

수질암에서는 pentagastrin이 calcitonin을 가장 잘 자극하는 반면, 정상인에서는 calcium 주입이 가장 잘 자극한다.

두 연구를 합치면 갑상선 수질암 환자 25명 중 2명만 pentagastrin에 정상 반응을 보였다. 많은 정상인이 pentagastrin에 과장된 반응을 보인 것으로 보고되었으며, 이 검사의 재현성은 낮다.

특히 가족력이 있으면 수술 치료를 고려해야 한다. 이환 가능성이 있는 가족 구성원은 65세까지 2년마다 선별검사를 받아야 한다.

참고문헌 (REFERENCES)

McLean et al., (1984) Metabolism 33:790

Karges et al., (2004) Exp Clin Endocrinol Diabetes 112:52

갑상선 수질암에 대한 calcium 주입 검사 (CALCIUM INFUSION TEST FOR MEDULLARY THYROID CANCER)

적응증 (INDICATIONS)

  1. Acalcitoninaemia 의심.
  2. 갑상선 수질암 의심.
  3. 갑상선 수질암 가족의 선별검사.
  4. MEN 2형 의심 환자.

금기 (CONTRAINDICATIONS)

출혈 질환.

부작용 (SIDE EFFECTS)

불쾌한 홍조감.

주요 부작용은 없다.

준비 (PREPARATION)

  • 환자는 밤새 금식한다.
  • 전해질과 혈청 calcium을 확인한다.
  • 정맥 cannula, 18–20 G.
  • 생리식염수 flush.
  • 10% calcium gluconate(10–20 mL 필요).
  • 빨간색 또는 노란색 튜브 6개.
  • 주사기.
  • 얼음과 검체를 즉시 검사실로 보낼 수 있는 시설.

방법 (METHOD)

  1. Cannula를 삽입하고 flush한다.
  2. Calcitonin 기저 검체를 채취한다.
  3. Calcium gluconate 0.2 mL/kg 체중을 1분에 걸쳐 정맥 투여한다.
  4. Cannula를 flush한다.
  5. 1, 2, 3, 5, 10분에 calcitonin 검체를 채취한다.
  6. 원심분리와 냉동을 위해 얼음에 담아 즉시 검사실로 보낸다.

해석 (INTERPRETATION)

  • 갑상선 수질암에서는 공복 혈청 calcitonin이 흔히 상승해 있으나(>90 ng/L) 정상 범위일 수도 있다.
  • 유발 검사는 calcitonin 측정의 민감도를 높인다.
  • Calcium 주입 후 최고 calcitonin의 정상 범위는 100–200 ng/L이다.

민감도와 특이도 (SENSITIVITY AND SPECIFICITY)

아래 인용한 연구에서 갑상선 수질암 환자 12명 중 8명이 calcium 주입에 증가된 반응을 보였다. 반응하지 않은 4명 중 2명은 기저 calcitonin이 상승해 있었다. 특히 젊은 남성에서 위양성률이 높다. 이 경우에는 pentagastrin 검사가 더 낫다.

참고문헌 (REFERENCES)

Gharib et al., (1987) Mayo Clinic Proc 62:373

개정 이력 (VERSION HISTORY)

John Wilding 12/89, Ben Jones 11/13

MEN-2 유전자 선별검사 (GENETIC SCREENING FOR MEN-2)

현재 지역 방침은 MEN2 진단이 의심되는 모든 사람을 내분비/유전 합동 클리닉에 의뢰하는 것이다(지역 환자는 직접 의뢰 가능, 지역 외 환자는 GP에게 의뢰를 요청). 유전 상담과 검체 채취는 클리닉에서 담당한다.

MEN1과 MEN2 유전자 선별검사를 하는 대안 검사실이 Exeter University에 있다.

임상 문의: Professor Andrew Hattersley. Tel: +44 (0) 1392 408260. Fax: 01392 403027. E-mail: A.T.Hattersley@ex.ac.uk

검사실 문의: Professor Sian Ellard. Tel: +44 (0) 1392 408529. Email: Sian.Ellard@nhs.net

의뢰서는 http://www.imperialendo.co.uk/men.htm 에서 받을 수 있다.

개정 이력 (VERSION HISTORY)

Karim Meeran 01/03, Niamh Martin 9/08, Saira Hameed 03/15

갑상선 결절 세침흡인 (FINE NEEDLE ASPIRATION OF A THYROID NODULE)

적응증 (INDICATION)

갑상선 결절의 평가. FNA의 주된 목적은 악성 배제이다.

방법 (METHOD)

  1. 암이 의심되는 모든 갑상선 결절은 Hammersmith Thyroid Clinic(클리닉 코드 THCI/THEM)의 Prof Graham Williams에게 의뢰한다. Mr Palazzo도 같은 날 Prof Williams와 병행하여 클리닉을 운영한다.
  2. 환자가 생화학적으로 정상 갑상선 상태이면 의뢰 시점에 초음파 유도 FNA를 미리 준비해 두는 것이 유용할 수 있다.

해석과 조치 (INTERPRETATION AND ACTION)

Guidelines for the management of Thyroid Cancer. British Thyroid Association (2014) Clinical Endocrinology 81(S1) 1-122

Thy 등급 세포학적 기술 조치
1 진단 불가(Non-diagnostic) 진단에 적합한 검체를 얻기 위해 FNA를 반복한다.
2

비종양성(Non-neoplastic)

세포가 충분하며 정상 갑상선 조직, 콜로이드 결절 또는 갑상선염 같은 비종양성 병변을 시사하는 검체.

콜로이드가 풍부한 낭종 검체는 Thy2c로 분류할 수 있다.

임상 및 영상(초음파) 소견과 대조한다. 의심스러운 임상/영상 소견이 동반되지 않으면 추가 생검은 필요 없다.
3

종양 가능성(Neoplasm possible)

다음으로 세분한다.

Thy3a — 비정형 소견이 있으나 다른 범주에 넣기에는 불충분한 경우.

Thy3f — 여포성 종양이 의심되는 경우. 이때 조직학적 가능성은 과형성 결절, 여포성 선종 또는 여포암이다. 세포검사만으로는 이들을 구별할 수 없으며, 진단을 위해 조직 검체(예: 진단적 반측 갑상선절제술)가 필요하다.

ICHNT MDT에서 논의

Thy3a — 추가 평가, 대개 초음파 평가와 FNAC 반복

Thy3f — 진단적 반측 갑상선절제술

4 악성 의심(Suspicious for malignancy)

ICHNT MDT에서 논의

동결절편 여부와 관계없이 진단적 반측 갑상선절제술, 또는 의심 정도가 특히 높으면 갑상선 전절제술

5 악성 진단(Diagnostic of malignancy)

ICHNT MDT에서 논의

종양 유형에 맞는 치료, 유두암이나 수질암은 대개 수술

West London Cancer Network 갑상선암 MDT 의뢰 방법 (HOW TO REFER TO THE WEST LONDON CANCER NETWORK THYROID CANCER MDT)

정보: http://www.londoncanceralliance.nhs.uk/information-for-healthcare-professionals/forms,-protocols-and-guidance/north-west-london-cancer-network/

연락처: http://www.imperial.nhs.uk/services/cancerservices/thyroidcancer/index.htm

갑상선 FNA 시행 (PERFORMING A THYROID FNA)

환자를 위 클리닉으로 의뢰하므로 현재는 두 병원 모두 일반 내분비 클리닉에서 시행하지 않는다. 역사적 목적으로 아래에 시술 방법을 요약한다.

준비 (PREPARATION)

없음.

부작용 (SIDE EFFECTS)

드물게 국소 출혈. 국소 압박으로 예방한다.

방법 (METHOD)

  1. 세포병리과에 연락하면 준비된 슬라이드를 가지고 거의 즉시 온다.
  2. 목을 뒤로 젖힌 누운 자세로 환자를 눕힌다.
  3. 25 G 바늘을 결절에 삽입하여 흡인한다(대개 여러 번 찌름). 대개 국소마취가 필요하다. 바늘은 단지 넣었다 뺐다만 해야 한다. 뒤로 당기면(흡인하면) 혈액이 섞인 검체가 나온다.
영어 원문 보기 — THYROID

THYROID

 

PROTOCOL FOR RADIO-IODINE TREATMENT

 

A recent study carried out in our centre demonstrated that the likelihood of cure following radio-iodine treatment was 60% higher in hyperthyroid patients that received adjuvant lithium (Martin et al., (2012) Clin Endo 77:621). The protocol below includes lithium, however the use of lithium is not essential and the protocol can be followed with lithium omitted.

Day –7 Thyroid function tests.  Information session & signing of consent form in Department of Nuclear Medicine, Charing Cross Hospital.  Send information letter to patient’s GP. Stop anti-thyroid medication.
Day -3 Start Lithium carbonate (Priadel) 800 mg nocte.
Day 0 Thyroid function tests.  Lithium level.  Radio-iodine treatment. 
Week +1 Thyroid function tests.  Lithium level.  STOP lithium treatment.
Week +3 Thyroid function tests.  Lithium level.
Week +6 Thyroid function tests.
Week +9 Thyroid function tests.
Week +12 Thyroid function tests.
Week +14 Outpatient clinic visit.
WHAT DO YOU NEED TO GIVE THE PATIENT?

1.      Information sheet on Radio-iodine treatment available at

http://www.imperialendo.com/for-patients/patent-information

2.      Give the patient an appointment for list Meeran D5 Endo Radioiodine F/UP at Charing Cross. Everything else (below) is for information only, and will be carried out from that clinic.

3.       

They will be given:

1. An X-Ray request form to Nuclear Medicine for I-131 Therapy.

2. Seven (7) request forms for TFTs (TSH, fT4, fT3 :Weeks –1, 0, +1, +3, +6, +9, +12 as above).

4.      2 request forms for a lithium level (week 0 and +1 as above).

5.      A prescription for lithium 800 mg (specify Priadel) nocte for 10 days.

6.      A prescription for thyroxine 100 μcg, NOT to be started until they are telephoned.

7.      An appointment for the out-patient clinic, 14 weeks after treatment.

8.      Make sure to note the patients’ telephone number in the notes and on the X-Ray form.  If they need to ring and enquire regarding results/treatment they should be given the switchboard number and bleep4998.

 

PROTOCOL FOR THE POST-RADIOIODINE TREATMENT TELEPHONE CLINIC

AIM

Our aim following radio-iodine treatment is to render patients euthyroid with hypothyroidism acceptable.  The purpose of the telephone clinic is to rapidly determine when thyroxine replacement should be started, thus avoiding unnecessary outpatient appointments or leaving patients with untreated hypothyroidism, which has many undesirable effects, including possible worsening of thyroid eye disease. See http://radioiodine.info.

 

WHO IS SUITABLE FOR TELEPHONE FOLLOW-UP?

This service is only available to patients who;

•        Are contactable by telephone during office hours (9am – 5pm).

•        Speak reasonable English and are able to follow instructions regarding medications.

•        Consent to regular blood tests, preferably at the hospital phlebotomy department.

 

HOW DO I ARRANGE FOR RADIO-IODINE TREATMENT?

If you think that a patient might be suitable for radioiodine they should be referred to Dr Zarni Win (Consultant Radiologist, Charing Cross Hospital) by cc-ing him a copy of your clinic letter or sending him an email (zarni.win@imperial.nhs.uk). This does not mean that the patient will have radioiodine but they should have an expert consultation to discuss this treatment option. In particular, radiation protection issues are not generalisable and should therefore be discussed with each patient on a case by case basis.

Anti-thyroid drugs should be stopped as soon as the decision to use radioiodine is made. Ideally they will be stopped for at least a week. If you know roughly when the patient wants radioiodine, further planning can be made. If it is clear that the patient will defer treatment for more than 2 months, then the antithyroid drugs should be continued until closer to the date of radioiodine. As a guide, the antithyroid drugs should be stopped at least 1 week and at most 2 months before radioiodine. Beta-blockers can be used to control symptoms.

If patient has active thyroid eye disease, see also section on thyroid eye disease for information on steroid cover for radioiodine treatment.

Give the patient an appointment for list Meeran D5 Endo Radioiodine F/UP at Charing CrossHospital as soon as possible. If you are at Hammersmith Hospital, you will need to give the patient an appointment on Meeran D5 Endo Radioiodine F/UP and also write on the frontsheet to actually book this on the Charing Cross system.

Send a referral letter (or fill in web form on http://meeran.info) to:

Radioiodine clinic (c/o Prof. Meeran),

c/o Dept of Endocrinology

9th Floor, East Wing, Charing Cross Hospital (Fax: 020 33 111862).

Make sure to include as many contact telephone numbers as possible in the letter.

 

WHO WILL FOLLOW PATIENTS UP BY TELEPHONE?

All computerised results are updated and reviewed on a weekly basis by a designated Doctor and patients telephoned if they become either biochemically hypo- or hyperthyroid following treatment.  

 

WHAT HAPPENS IF A PATIENT DEVELOPS SIGNIFICANT SYMPTOMS AFTER RADIOIODINE?

If at any stage a patient becomes symptomatic or develops Grave’s ophthalmopathy the Department of Endocrinology can be contacted by either the patient or the GP and arrangements made to review the patient urgently at the next outpatient clinic.

WHEN WILL THYROXINE BE STARTED?

Blood samples will be checked on the day of radioiodineI, and 1 week later for lithium levels. Thyroid function tests will also be monitored, but no action taken (as the fT4 may be lowered by the lithium).

When the fT4 falls to 14.5 pmol/Lor less, then thyroxine will be commenced by telephone. Samples will be collected at 3, 6, 9 and 12 weeks, and the patient attend clinic at about 14 weeks. If they are still toxic, further telephone appointments can be made at 15, 18 and 21 weeks.

 

If at review (after 12 weeks), the TSH is still not detectable, then this is either due to excess thyroxine, or due to failed radioiodine. A fT3 > 5 pmol/Lsuggests autonomy, and the thyroxine should be discontinued. If the fT3 < 5.0 pmol/L, then the dose of thyroxine can be slowly lowered with 3 weekly phone reviews.

 

INSTRUCTIONS FOR THOSE RUNNING THE TELEPHONE CLINIC

 

Patients will be expecting phonecalls to tell them whether or not to start thyroxine.

 

First look on computer for results of fT3/ fT4/TSH.

 

Ask the patient their weight in kilograms (or stone/lbs and convert).

 

Find out how they feel. Ask specifically about nausea. Check that they have been compliant with the lithium. How many tablets did they take? Which ones did they miss?

 

If the patient feels toxic (tremor, tachycardia etc), and the fT4 > 40 pmol/L, then suggest propranolol e.g. 20-40 mg tds daily until next (3 weeks) review, as they might still rapidly become hypothyroid.

 

If fT4 < 14.6 pmol/L, then start thyroxine 100 mcg daily. The patient should have a supply of these at home, and should be able to start these the day you make the phonecall.

 

After week 12, if TSH suppressed, then check fT3. If fT3 > 5 pmol/L, then stop thyroxine. If fT3 < 5pmol/L, then reduce thyroxine slowly.

 

This page to be filled in with each phonecall in the thyroid clinic made and stored in the thyroid file.

 

Name:

 

Hospital Number:

 

Date of phone call:

 

Date of blood test:

Weeks since RAI:

 

Results:            fT4

 

                        fT3

 

                        TSH

Lithium level checked (yes/no). Do NOT get result.

Do you smoke?  How many per day?

 

What tablets are you CURRENTLY taking:

 

If fT4 < 14.6 pmol/L, then start thyroxine 100 mcg once daily. Advice given: start thyroxine or wait until further samples taken:

 

If fT4 > 30pmol/Land patient feels unwell (tremor, tachycardia etc), then consider propranolol.

 

After week 12, if TSH suppressed, then check fT3. If fT3 > 5 pmol/L, then stop thyroxine. If fT3 < 5 pmol/L, then reduce thyroxine slowly.

 

THYROID EYE DISEASE AND RADIOIODINE

 

Worsening of thyroid eye disease mainly occurs in smokers, and they have an approximately 25% risk of worsening opthalmopathy. In a large RCT, smokers had an increased risk of progression or de novo development of thyroid eye disease independently of the treatment modality for hyperthyroidism, but smokers treated with radio-iodine had the highest risk. Low doses of prednisolone (0.2 mg/kg body weight), started 1 day after radio-iodine administration and continued for 6 weeks, were shown to be effective with very little side effects in a retrospective matched cohort study. It is preferable to avoid steroids in the majority, and treat the few who develop worsening eye signs with steroids at that stage.

 

There is thus no indication for the use of prophylactic steroids in non-smokers. If there is any doubt, all patients should be referred to Vickie Lee (Consultant Ophthalmologist at Charing Cross Hospital).

 

REFERENCES

Bartalena et al., (1998) Ann Int Med 129:632

(Traisk F,et al., (2009) J Clin Endocrinol Metab 94:3700

Lai et al., (2010) J Clin Endocrinol Metab 95:1333

PREPARATION OF THYROTOXIC PATIENTS FOR THYROIDECTOMY

 

Patients will ideally be rendered euthyroid before surgery. In all cases, one must always balance the risks of delay against the benefit of being euthyroid pre-operatively. In an emergency, the following combination of drugs can be used to try and achieve this in approximately ten days:

1. Beta blockade. Propranolol 80 mg tds is favoured historically. Atenolol 100 mg or Nadolol 16 0mg can be administered only once daily and hence might improve compliance

2. Propylthiouracil (PTU) 250 mg qds. This is a higher dose than used normally, but in emergency higher doses more frequently are required in view of the fact that the liver increases the metabolism of these drugs. The PTU should be administered an hour before any iodide treatment as the PTU will prevent organification of the administered iodine.

3. An excess of iodide or iodine (KI 65 mg tds or 0.3 ml Lugols iodine tds). Potassium iodide 65mg tds is by far the easiest to administer and is not foul tasting like Lugol’s NEJM (1980) 302;883-885)

STARTING THYROXINE POST-TOTAL OR COMPLETION THYROIDECTOMY

 

After a total or completion thyroidectomy we commence thyroxine based on a weight-guided estimation. The typical dose is 1.5-2 mcg/Kg. In the elderly we err towards 1.5mcg/Kg to minimise the risk of atrial fibrillation‎.

 

 

PROTOCOL FOR THE MANAGEMENT OF THYROID EYE DISEASE (TED)

 

REFER URGENTLY TO OPHTHALMOLOGY IF ANY OF THE FOLLOWING ARE PRESENT

Symptoms:

•       Unexplained deterioration in vision

•       Awareness of change in intensity/quality of colour vision in one or both eyes

•       History of eye(s) suddenly ‘popping out’ (globe subluxation)

Signs:

•       Obvious corneal opacity

•       Cornea still visible when eyelids are closed

•       Optic disc swelling

 

REFER NON-URGENTLY TO OPHTHALMOLOGY IF ANY OF THE FOLLOWING ARE PRESENT

Symptoms:

•        Eyes abnormally sensitive to light (photophobia): troublesome or deteriorating over past 12 months

•        Eyes excessively gritty and not improving after 1 week of topical lubricant

•        Pain in or behind the eyes: troublesome or deteriorating over past 12 months

•        Progressive change in appearance of the eyes and/or eyelids over the past 1-2 months

•        Diplopia

Signs:

•        Troublesome eyelid retraction

•        Abnormal swelling or redness of eyelid(s) or conjunctiva

•        Restriction of eye movements or manifest strabismus

•        Tilting of the head to avoid double vision

 

CLINICAL ACTIVITY SCORE (CAS)

Pain at rest-1

Pain on Extra Ocular Eye Movement-1  

Redness of the conjunctiva-1

Redness of the eyelids-1

Swelling of the caruncle-1

Swelling of the conjunctiva (chemosis)-1

Swelling of the eyelid (oedema)- 1

 

CAS of > 3 indicates active disease likely to warrant first line therapy

 

SEVERITY GRADING

Grade 1            - Ocular irritation

Grade 2            - Soft tissue swelling or proptosis

Grade 3            - Any restriction of Extra Ocular Movement

Grade 4            - Optic Neuropathy or Exposure Keratopathy

 

MANAGEMENT PROTOCOL (from EUGOGO)

 

 

GENERAL MANAGEMENT OF TED

•        Smoking cessation programme

•        Restore euthyroidism

•        Monitor thyroid status closely

•        If thyroid eye disease is active radioiodine treatment should be accompanied by steroid cover which should be started on the day that radioiodine is administered:

o   We advocate a prednisolone at a dose of 0.2mg/kg.

o   Continue this same dose for 6 weeks and stop dead (ie no tapering)

•        Inactive stable thyroid eye disease does not require steroid cover

•        Avoid post radioiodine hypothyroidism

•        Selenium supplementation

 

MILD TED (GRADE I/II SEVERITY)

•       Symptoms

o   Ocular irritation with signs of mild lid retraction and or proptosis (<3 mm).

•       Mild activity where clinical activity score is below 3/7 including mild soft tissue swelling (intermittent diplopia, saccadic discord).

•       If active, does not usually warrant systemic immunosuppression and can often be managed with improvememt of the ocular surface.

•       Regarding choice of lubricant Clinitas, Hyloforte, Xailin HA are all appropriate.

•       If you are stuck, simple carmellose (Celluvisc for example) will do. For night time paraffin based ointment such as Xailin Night or Lacrimal Lube are good.

 

MODERATE TO SEVERE TED (GRADE III SEVERITY)

•       Clinical activity >3/7:

•       Proptosis >3 mm, soft tissue swelling (oedema or erythema of lids, conjunctival chemosis or injection and caruncular swelling), pain (at rest on or extraocular movements), diplopia (constant, inconstant).

•       If active, warrants systemic immunosuppression (e.g. intravenous glucocorticoid – see below) when significant impact on activities of daily living.

•       Inactive disease may warrant surgical rehabilitation (lid lowering, strabismus surgery or orbital decompression)

 

SIGHT THREATENING TED (GRADE IV)

Optic neuropathy or corneal breakdown secondary to exposure keratopathy requires immediate intervention – medical or surgical decompression.

MEDICAL MANAGEMENT

The below is for information/guidance only.

All systemic immunosuppression must be guided by ophthalmology colleagues with a specialist interest in TED with support from rheumatology when appropriate.

Pulsed methylprednisolone 1 g i.v. daily for three consecutive days (usually on PIU) then continue either 250 mg i.v. weekly or oral prednisolone 0.7-1 mg/kg.

+/- 2nd line immunosuppression.

 

FOR MODERATE TO SEVERE TED

Pulsed methylprednisolone 500 mg i.v. weekly for 6 weeks followed by 250 mg weekly for further 6 weeks. Not suitable in children.

Early response to steroid predicts treatment outcome. Outcome is inversely related to duration of disease.

Depending on severity 2nd line immunosuppression may be considered by ophthalmology with rheumatology support.

RADIOTHERAPY

Although radiotherapy in combination with steroids is more effective in treating active thyroid eye disease than steroids alone, it is not known if 2nd line immunosuppression in combination with steroid is more effective than radiotherapy combined with steroid. The results of the CIRTED study will perhaps provide further evidence supporting one or the other treatment regime.

SURGICAL MANAGEMENT

Orbital decompression.

 

REFERENCES

EUGOGO (2008) Eur J Endocrinol 158:273

Bartlena et al., (1997) Clin Endocrinol (Oxf) 47:9

Mourits et al., (2011) New Engl J Med 364:1920

Mano Sira 04/15

 

PENTAGASTRIN TEST FOR MEDULLARY THYROID CARCINOMA

 

CALCITONIN (CT)

Calcitonin may be secreted by C-cells on the thyroid gland. High levels may suggest medullary thyroid carcinoma. In very early disease (e.g. on screening for familial syndromes), levels may not be raised, but may be stimulated either by calcium or pentagastrin. It is suggested that borderline baseline levels (which no longer need be fasting) are further investigated by a stimulation test.

INDICATIONS

1.      Suspected medullary carcinoma of thyroid.

2.      Screening of families with medullary carcinoma of the thyroid.

3.      Patients with suspected MEN type 2. Remember to inform the MEN2 registry.

4.      Patients with basal CT level (< 11.8 (male) and < 4.8(female) ngLl) /L/L

 

CONTRAINDICATIONS

Allergy/anaphylaxis

SIDE EFFECTS

Severe short lasting nausea, epigastric discomfort

PREPARATION

1.      Check electrolytes and serum calcium.

2.      i.v. Cannula, 18-20g.

3.      Pentagastrin 0.5 mcg/kg body weight. Pentagastrin is available as 500mcg in 2ml. So for a 100kg patient, you need 50mcg, which is only 0.2ml.

4.      6 x red or yellow top tubes.

5.      Centrifuge.

6.      Ice or facilities to transfer samples immediately to lab

METHOD

1.      Patients should be fasted, as food can increase calcitonin.

2.      Insert cannula and flush.

3.      Take baseline sample for calcitonin.

4.      Ensure the patient is lying down before administering pentagastrin, which makes some patients feel very unwell for a few seconds.

5.      Give bolus i.v. of pentagastrin 0.5 mcg/kg body weight and flush cannula.

6.      Take samples at 1, 2, 3, 5 and 10 minutes for calcitonin.

7.      Take immediately to lab on ice.

INTERPRETATION

In patients with a stimulated CT 30-100 ng/L- follow-up screening recommended.

In patients with a stimulated CT 100-200 ng/L- probable C-cell hyperplasia or early MTC.

In patients with a stimulated CT >200 ng/L- MTC very likely.

SENSITIVITY AND SPECIFICITY

Pentagastrin stimulates calcitonin best in medullary carcinoma, whereas calcium infusion is best in normals.

Combining two studies only two out of 25 patients with medullary thyroid carcinoma had normal responses to pentagastrin. Many normals have been described with an exaggerated response to pentagastrin and the reproducibility of this test is poor.

Surgical treatment should be considered especially if there is a family history. Potentially affected family members should be screened biennially until aged 65 years.

 

REFERENCES

McLean et al., (1984) Metabolism 33:790

Karges et al., (2004) Exp Clin Endocrinol Diabetes 112:52

 

 

CALCIUM INFUSION TEST FOR MEDULLARY THYROID CANCER

INDICATIONS

1.      Suspected acalcitoninaemia.

2.      Suspected medullary carcinoma of thyroid.

3.      Screening of families with medullary carcinoma of the thyroid.

4.      Patients with suspected MEN type 2.

CONTRAINDICATIONS

Bleeding disorders

SIDE EFFECTS

Unpleasant flushing sensation

No major side effects

PREPARATION

•       Patient should fast overnight.

•       Check electrolytes and serum calcium.

•       i.v. Cannula, 18-20g.

•       Saline flush.

•       Calcium gluconate 10% (10 - 20 ml required).

•       6 x red or yellow tubes.

•       Syringes.

•       Ice and facilities to transfer samples immediately to lab.

METHOD

1.      Insert cannula and flush.

2.      Take baseline sample for calcitonin.

3.      Give calcium gluconate 0.2 ml/kg body weight i.v. over 1 minute.

4.      Flush cannula.

5.      Take samples at 1, 2, 3, 5 and 10 minutes for calcitonin.

6.      Send immediately on ice to the lab for centrifugation and freezing.

INTERPRETATION

•       In medullary carcinoma of the thyroid there is often a raised fasting serum calcitonin (>90 ng/\L) but this may be in the normal range.

•       Provocative tests improve the sensitivity of calcitonin measurement.

•       Normal range for peak calcitonin following calcium infusion is 100 to 200 ng/L.

SENSITIVITY AND SPECIFICITY

In the study quoted below 8/12 subjects with medullary thyroid carcinoma had increased responses to calcium infusion. Two of the four who failed to respond had a raised baseline calcitonin. There is a high false positive rate especially in young men. The pentagastrin test is better in this situation.

REFERENCES

Charib et al., (1987) Mayo Clinic Proc 62:373

John Wilding 12/89, Ben Jones 11/13

 

GENETIC SCREENING FOR MEN-2

 

The current local policy is referral of anyone in whom a diagnosis of MEN2 is suspected to the joint Endocrine/Genetics clinic (can be done directly for local patients for out of area patients request a referral from the GP). Genetic counselling and sampling will be handled by the clinic.

 

An alternative laboratory for MEN1 and MEN2 genetic screening runs at Exeter University

FINE NEEDLE ASPIRATION OF A THYROID NODULE

INDICATION

Investigation of thyroid nodule(s). The prime aim of FNA is to exclude malignancy.

METHOD
METHOD

1.      All thyroid nodules suspicious of cancer should be referred to the Hammersmith Thyroid Clinic (clinic code THCI/THEM) to Prof Graham Williams or Mr Palazzo also has a clinic in parallel with Prof Williams on the same day.

2.      It may be useful to arrange an US guided FNA at the time of the referral so long as the patient is biochemically euthyroid.

 

 

 

 

 

 

 

 

 

 

 

INTERPRETATION AND ACTION

Guidelines for the management of Thyroid Cancer. British Thyroid Association (2014) Clinical Endocrinology 81(S1) 1-122

Thy Grade Cytological description Action taken

1

 

 

Non-diagnostic Repeat FNA to obtain a sample that is adequate for diagnosis.
2

Non-neoplastic

Samples are adequately cellular and suggest a non-neoplastic lesion such as normal thyroid tissue, a colloid nodule or a thyroidits.

Cyst samples containing abundant colloid can be categorized as Thy2c

Correlate with clinical and radiological (US) findings. If no suspisious associated clinical/radiological findings then no further biopsy required.
3

Neoplasm possible

This is subdivided into:

Thy3a – when there are atypical features present but not enough to place into any of the other categories.

 

Thy3f – when a follicular neoplasm is suspected. The histological possibilities then include a hyperplastic nodule, follicular adenoma or follicular carcinoma. These cannot be distinguished on cytology alone and a histology sample (e.g. diagnostic hemithyroidectomy) will be required for diagnosis.

 

DISCUSS IN ICHNT MDT

 

Thy3a – further investigation, usually US assessment and repeat FNAC

 

Thy3f – diagnostic hemithyroidectomy

4 Suspicious for malignancy

DISCUSS IN ICHNT MDT

Diagnostic hemithyroidectomy with or without frozen section or total thyroidectomy when the degree of suspicion is particularly high

5 Diagnostic of malignancy

DISCUSS IN ICHNT MDT

 

Therapy appropriate to tumour type, usually surgery for papillary or medullary thyroid carcinomas

HOW TO REFER TO THE WEST LONDON CANCER NETWORK THYROID CANCER MDT

For information see: http://www.londoncanceralliance.nhs.uk/information-for-healthcare-professionals/forms,-protocols-and-guidance/north-west-london-cancer-network/

 

Contact details available from: http://www.imperial.nhs.uk/services/cancerservices/thyroidcancer/index.htm

 

VERSION HISTORY

Stephen Gilbey 10/89, Karim Meeran 07/01, Tricia Tan 8/08, Florian Wernig and Chioma Izzi-Engbeaya 11/13, Saira Hameed 02/15, Fausto Palzzo 10/15

 

 

PERFORMING A THYROID FNA

This is no longer done in the general endocrine clinics at either site as patients are referred to the clinics above. Outlined below is the procedure for historical purposes.

PREPARATION

None

SIDE EFFECTS

Rarely: local bleeding. Avoid by applying local pressure

METHOD

1.      Contact cytology department who will come almost immediately and bring their prepared slides.

2.      Lie patient in supine position with neck flexed backwards.

3.      Insert 25 gauge needle into nodule and aspirate (usually more than one pass). Local anaesthesia is usually necessary.  The needle should just be passed in and out.  Don't draw back as this results in a bloody tap.

 

10번역 완료

부갑상선 (PARATHYROID)

부갑상선기능항진증 (HYPERPARATHYROIDISM)

부갑상선기능항진증의 진단 (DIAGNOSIS OF HYPERPARATHYROIDISM)

  • 보정 Ca2+ 상승.
  • PO43- 저하.
  • Alkaline phosphatase 정상.
  • 혈청 PTH 정상 또는 상승.
  • Vitamin D 결핍 배제.
  • 24시간 소변 Ca2+ 배설 증가.
  • 가족성 저칼슘뇨성 고칼슘혈증(FHH)을 배제하려면 calcium clearance 대 creatinine clearance 비가 >0.01이어야 한다. 계산법은 다음과 같다(간편 계산은 온라인 계산기 https://www.impendo.co.uk/for-doctors/toolkit-for-doctors 참조).

Calcium clearance

[소변 calcium (mmol/L) × 소변량 (mL)] / [혈청 calcium (mmol/L) × 1440]

Creatinine clearance (이미 계산되어 있을 수 있다)

[소변 creatinine (mmol/L) × 소변량 (mL)] / [혈청 creatinine (mmol/L) × 1440]

혈청 creatinine은 보통 µmol/L로 표시되므로 1000으로 나누어 mmol/L로 환산해야 한다.

그러나 이 비는 다음과 같이 단순화할 수 있다.

비 = [소변 calcium (mmol/L) × (혈청 creatinine (µmol/L) / 1000)] ÷ [혈청 calcium (mmol/L) × 소변 creatinine (mmol/L)]

예시

FHH

  • 소변 calcium 1.0 mmol/L
  • 소변 creatinine 6.2 mmol/L
  • 혈청 creatinine 130 µmol/L
  • 혈청 calcium 2.65 mmol/L
  • 비 = [1.0 × (130/1000)] ÷ [2.65 × 6.2] = 0.0079

원발성 부갑상선기능항진증 (Primary Hyperparathyroidism)

  • 소변 calcium 2.2 mmol/L
  • 소변 creatinine 1.4 mmol/L
  • 혈청 creatinine 74 µmol/L
  • 혈청 calcium 3.3 mmol/L
  • 비 = [2.2 × (74/1000)] ÷ [3.3 × 1.4] = 0.035

부갑상선 수술을 고려할 대상 (WHO TO CONSIDER FOR PARATHYROID SURGERY)

  • 모든 증상이 있는 환자(신경정신 증상, 신결석증, 골질환, 고혈압을 포함한 심혈관질환).
  • 수술로 이득을 볼 수 있는 무증상 환자는 다음과 같다.
    • 질환 진행 위험이 있는 환자.
    • 부갑상선절제술 후 호전될 수 있는 질환 특징을 가진 환자.

제4차 무증상 원발성 부갑상선기능항진증 관리 국제 워크숍(2014)은 수술로 이득을 볼 수 있는 무증상 환자의 특징으로 다음을 제시했다.

[역자 주] 원문에는 “Primary Hyperthyroidism”으로 적혀 있으나 hyperparathyroidism의 오기이다.

J Clin Endocrinol Metab (2014) 99(10):3561-3569

  • Age(나이) <50세.
  • Bone density(골밀도): 고관절, 요추 또는 원위 요골에서 최대 골량보다 2.5 표준편차 넘게 낮음(T score <−2.5) 및/또는 이전 취약 골절.
  • Calcium 농도가 정상 상한보다 ≥0.25 mmol/L 높음(즉 2.85 mmol/L).
  • Creatinine clearance <60 mL/min(또는 24시간 소변 calcium 배설 >10 mmol/day이면서 결석 형성 위험 증가).

부갑상선 선종의 위치 확인 (LOCALISATION OF PARATHYROID ADENOMA)

위치 확인 검사는 환자에게 부갑상선 수술을 고려하는 경우에만 필요하다.

어떤 기법도 신뢰할 만하지 않아 흔히 여러 방법을 병용한다.

  • 경부 초음파.
  • Sesta-MIBI scan.
  • 대개 추가 검사는 하지 않으나, 일부 환자는 4DCT 경부(+ 상종격동)를 시행할 수 있다.
  • PTH 선택적 정맥 채혈은 일상적으로 사용하지 않으며 재수술에만 쓴다. 환자는 입원해야 하며, 검사는 Dr Alsafi에게 충분히 미리 예약해야 한다.
  • 수술이 필요한 모든 원발성 부갑상선기능항진증 환자는 내분비외과의 Mr Fausto Palazzo 또는 Mr Neil Tolley에게 의뢰한다.

부갑상선절제술 전 관리 (MANAGEMENT PRIOR TO PARATHYROIDECTOMY)

  • 환자가 40세 미만인가? MEN-1일 가능성이 있는가? 그렇다면 MEN-1 돌연변이 선별검사를 논의한다.
  • U&Es, Ca2+, PO4, alkaline phosphatase, albumin을 포함한 통상적 수술 전 혈액검사.
  • 적절한 수분 공급을 유지한다.
  • 결손량을 보충하고 하루 3–4 L의 수액을 정맥으로 유지하며, 환자가 마실 수 있으면 경구로 전환한다.
  • 위 조치로 보정 Ca2+가 <3.0 mmol/L로 낮아지지 않으면 bisphosphonate를 투여한다(예: pamidronate 30 mg을 0.9% saline 1 L에 섞어 4시간에 걸쳐, 또는 zoledronic acid 4 mg을 5% glucose 또는 0.9% sodium chloride 100 mL에 섞어 15분에 걸쳐 정맥 투여). 24시간이 지나야 효과가 나타나기 시작하며 최대 효과는 5–6일에 나타난다. Bisphosphonate 치료 후 심한 수술 후 저칼슘혈증을 피하도록 미리 계획한다.

Vitamin D 결핍과 hungry bone에 관한 참고 (A NOTE ON VITAMIN D DEFICIENCY AND HUNGRY BONES)

Vitamin D 결핍은 hungry bone syndrome 발생의 위험인자이다. 이 증후군은 중증 부갑상선기능항진증으로 부갑상선절제술을 받은 후 뚜렷한 저칼슘혈증(보정 Ca2+ <2.1 mmol/L)으로 나타난다. 기전은 불분명하나, PTH 자극이 갑자기 중단되면서 빠른 골 재광화가 일어나 순환 calcium이 뼈로 격리되는 것으로 생각된다. 높은 PTH에 의한 만성적 골흡수 증가로 수술 전부터 골질환이 있던 환자에서 가장 흔하다. 부갑상선절제술 전에 vitamin D 농도를 정상화할 것을 권한다. Vitamin D 보충에 관한 정보: http://www.imperialendo.com/for-doctors/vitamin-d-guidelines

부갑상선절제술 후 관리 (MANAGEMENT AFTER PARATHYROIDECTOMY)

  • 저칼슘혈증 증상(감각이상, 근경련 등)을 묻는다.
  • U&Es, 보정 Ca2+, PTH를 수술 후 오후 9시와 오전 6시에 검사하여 외과 회진 때 결과가 나와 있게 한다.
  • 부갑상선절제술 후 증상성 저칼슘혈증은 드물다. 증상이 있고 보정 Ca2+ >1.9 mmol/L이면 발포성 Ca2+를 투여한다(Sandocal 1000 2정 1일 2회 권장, 대안으로 Adcal 3정 1일 2회 또는 Calcichew Forte 2정 1일 2회).
  • 부갑상선절제술 후 정맥 calcium은 거의 필요하지 않으며(Hammersmith Hospital에서 2005–2015년 원발성 부갑상선기능항진증에 대한 부갑상선절제술 1400건에서 한 번도 사용하지 않음), 고려한다면 담당 외과 전문의와 논의해야 한다. 증상이 심하고, PTH가 측정되지 않으며, 경구 약물에 반응하지 않고, 보정 Ca2+ <1.9 mmol/L이면 10% calcium gluconate 10–20 mL를 5% dextrose 50–100 mL에 섞어 ECG 모니터링하에 10분에 걸쳐 정맥 투여하는 것을 고려한다. 환자가 무증상이 될 때까지 반복할 수 있다.
  • 정맥 calcium 투여의 위험: 흔하지 않으나 국소 혈전정맥염, 심독성, 저혈압, calcium 맛, 홍조, 오심, 구토, 발한이 있다. 부정맥이 있거나 digoxin 치료 중인 환자는 정맥 calcium 보충 중 지속적 ECG 모니터링이 필요하다.
  • PTH를 모니터링한다. PTH가 측정되고 부갑상선 기능이 회복되면 alfacalcidol을 서서히 끊는다. PTH가 계속 측정되지 않으면 평생 alfacalcidol을 복용할 가능성이 높다. 이 경우 소변 calcium-phosphate 곱이 높아지므로 장기적 위험은 신석회화증이다. 따라서 장기 목표는 정상 하한의 calcium이다.

참고문헌 (REFERENCES)

Witteveen et al., (2013) Eur J Endocrinol.168:R45-53

Emergency Endocrine Guidance, Society for Endocrinology (2013) Acute Hypocalcaemia for use in adult patients.

개정 이력 (VERSION HISTORY)

Arshia Panahloo 1/98, Sarah Stanley 07/01 개정, Alexander Comninos and Saira Hameed 09/15 개정, Fausto Palazzo 10/15

영어 원문 보기 — PARATHYROID

PARATHYROID

HYPERPARATHYROIDISM

 

DIAGNOSIS OF HYPERPARATHYROIDISM

•        Elevated corrected Ca2+.

•        Low PO43-.

•        Normal alkaline phosphatase.

•        Normal or elevated serum PTH.

•        Exclude vitamin D deficiency

•        High 24 hr urinary Ca2+ excretion.

•        To exclude Familial Hypocalciuric Hypercalcaemia (FHH), the calcium clearance to creatinine clearance ratio should be > 0.01. This is calculated as follows (for easy calculation see https://www.impendo.co.uk/for-doctors/toolkit-for-doctors for on-line calculator).

 

Calcium Clearance

[Urine Calcium (mmol/L) x urine volume (ml)] / [ Serum calcium (mmol/L) x 1440]

 

Creatinine Clearance  (This may be calculated already)

[Urine Creatinine (mmol/L) x urine volume (ml)] / [ Serum creatinine (mmol/L) x 1440]

Serum creatinine is normally expressed in µmol/L and needs to be converted to mmol/Lby dividing by 1000.

 

However, this ratio can be reduced to:

 

Urine Calcium (mmol/L) x [Serum creatinine (µmol/L) / 1000]

Serum calcium (mmol/L) x Urine Creatinine (mmol/L)

For example

FHH

Urine calcium 1.0 mmol/L                                  Ratio = 1.0 x [130/1000] = 0.0079

Urine creatinine 6.2 mmol/L                          2.65 x 6.2

Serum creatinine 130 µmol/L

Serum calcium 2.65 mmol/L

 

Primary Hyperparathyroidism

Urine calcium 2.2mmol/L                                               Ratio = 2.2 x [74/1000] = 0.035

Urine creatinine 1.4 mmol/L                           3.3 x 1.4

Serum creatinine 74 µmol/L

Serum calcium 3.3 mmol/L

WHO TO CONSIDER FOR PARATHYROIDTHYROID SURGERY

 

•       All symptomatic patients (neuropsychiatric, nephrolithiasis, bone disease, cardiovascular disease including hypertension).

•       Asymptomatic patients who might benefit from surgery include:

o   Those at risk for disease progression,

o   Those who have features of the disease that may improve following parathyroidectomy.

 

The Fourth International Workshop on the Management of Asymptomatic Primary Hyperthyroidism (2014) identified the following features in asymptomatic patients who might benefit from surgery:

J Clin Endocrinol Metab (2014) 99(10):3561-3569

Age <50 years.

Bone density at the hip, lumbar spine, or distal radius > 2.5 standard deviations below peak bone mass (T score <-2.5) and/or previous fragility fracture.

Calcium concentration ≥ 0.25 mmol/Labove the upper limit of normal, (i.e.2.85mmol/L)

Creatinine clearance < 60 mL/min (or 24h urine calcium excretion >10 mmol/day with increased risk of stone formation).

 

LOCALISATION OF PARATHYROID ADENOMA

 

Localisation studies are only required if the patient is being considered for parathyroid surgery

None of the techniques are reliable and often a combination of methods are used.

•        Ultrasound of neck.

•        Sesta-MIBI scanning.

•        Usually no additional tests are performed but selected patients may undergo a 4DCT neck (+ upper mediastinum).

•        Selective venous sampling for PTH is not routinely used and reserved for re-operations only.  Patient requires hospital admission, and investigation needs to be booked with Dr Alsafi well in advance.

•        All patients with primary hyperparathyroidism needing surgery should be referred to the endocrine surgical department to Mr Fausto Palazzo or Mr Neil Tolley.

 

 

 

 

 

 

MANAGEMENT PRIOR TO PARATHYROIDECTOMY

 

•        Is the patient <40 years? Could the patient have MEN-1? If so discuss screening for MEN-1 mutations.

•      Usual pre-operative bloods, including U&Es, Ca2+, PO4, alkaline phosphatase, albumin.

•      Maintain adequate hydration.

•      Replace deficit and maintain 3-4 L fluids /day i.v. and then orally if patient able to drink.

•      If above measures do not reduce corrected Ca2+<3.0 mmol/Lgive a bisphosphonates (e.g. pamidronate 30 mg in 1 litre 0.9% saline over 4 hrs or IV zolendroic acid 4mg in 100ml 5% glucose or 0.9& sodium chloride over 15 minutes). This will not start to work for 24 hours, with maximum effect 5-6 days.  Plan in advance to avoid severe post-operative hypocalcaemia following bisphosphonate therapy.

A NOTE ON VITAMIN D DEFICIENCY AND HUNGRY BONES

Vitamin D deficiency is a risk factor for the development of the hungry bone syndrome which presents as marked hypocalcaemia (corrected Ca2+ < 2.1 mmol/L) following parathyroidectomy for severe hyperparathyroidism. The mechanism is unclear but it is thought that the abrupt cessation of the PTH stimulus results in rapid bone remineralisation sequestering circulating calcium into bone. It occurs most often in patients with pre-operative bone disease resulting from a chronic increase in bone resorption induced by high levels of PTH. It is recommended that Vitamin D levels be normalised prior to parathyroidectomy. For information on Vitmain D replacement see: http://www.imperialendo.com/for-doctors/vitamin-d-guidelines

 

MANAGEMENT AFTER PARATHYROIDECTOMY

•      Enquire for symptoms of hypocalcaemia (paraesthesiae, cramps etc.).

•      U&Es, corrected Ca2+ & PTH tested at 9pm post op and 6am so that results ready for surgical ward round.

•      Rarely are patients symptomatically hypocalcaemic after a parathyroidectomy. If symptoms occur and corrected Ca2+ > 1.9 mmol/l, give effervescent Ca2+ (Sandocal 1000 2 tablets bd suggested, alternatives include Adcal 3 tablets bd or cacichew forte 2 tablets bd).

•      Intravenous calcium is almost never required after a parathyroidectomy (never used in 1400 parathyroidectomies for primary hyperparathyroidism at Hammersmith Hospital since 2005-2015) and if considered should be discussed with the consultant surgeon. If the symptoms are severe, PTH unrecordable, the patient not responding to oral medication and the corrected Ca2+ <1.9 mmol/l consider 10-20 ml 10% calcium gluconate in 50-100ml of 5% dextrose i.v. over 10 minutes with ECG monitoring. This can be repeated until the patient is asymptomatic.

•      Hazards of i.v. calcium administration: uncommon but include local thrombophlebitis, cardiotoxicity, hypotension, calcium taste, flushing, nausea, vomiting and sweating. Patients with cardiac arrhythmias or on digoxin therapy need continuous ECG monitoring during i.v. calcium replacement.

•      Monitor PTH. If detectable and parathyroid recovery occurs, wean off alfacalcidol. If PTH remains undetectable, it is likely that they will stay on alfacalcidol for life. In that situation, the long term risk is nephrocalcinosis as the urinary calcium-phosphate product will be high. Thus long term aim is for a low-normal calcium.

 

REFERENCES

Witteveen et al., (2013) Eur J Endocrinol.168:R45-53

Emergency Endocrine Guidance, Society for Endocrinology (2013) Acute Hypocalcaemia for use in adult patients.
